### Parties and elections

Main articles: [Elections in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elections_in_Turkey), [Political parties in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_parties_in_Turkey), and [Electoral cycle of Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electoral_cycle_of_Turkey)

Recep Tayyip Erdoğan

[Recep Tayyip Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan) \
[President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Turkey)&#x20;

Cevdet Yılmaz

[Cevdet Yılmaz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cevdet_Y%C4%B1lmaz) \
[Vice President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vice_President_of_Turkey)&#x20;



Results of the [2024 Turkish local elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Turkish_local_elections)

Elections in Turkey are held for six functions of government: [presidential](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_presidential_elections) (national), parliamentary (national), [municipality mayors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_municipalities_in_Turkey) (local), district mayors (local), provincial or [municipal council members](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_municipalities_in_Turkey) (local), and [muhtars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhtar_\(title\)) (local). [Referendums](51582.md) are also held occasionally. Every Turkish citizen who has turned 18 has the [right to vote](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right_to_vote) and stand as a candidate at elections. [Universal suffrage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_suffrage) for both sexes has been applied throughout Turkey since 1934.[^cite_note-237] In Turkey, [turnout rates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voter_turnout) of both local and general elections are high compared to many other countries, which usually stands higher than 80%. President Recep Tayyip Erdoğan is currently serving as the [head of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_state) and [head of government](37570.md).[^cite_note-238][^cite_note-239] [Özgür Özel](49050282.md) is the [Main Opposition Leader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_the_Main_Opposition_Leaders_of_Turkey).&#x20;

The [electoral threshold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electoral_threshold) for political parties at national level is seven percent of the votes.[^cite_note-240] Smaller parties can avoid the electoral threshold by forming an alliance with other parties. [Independent candidates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_candidates) are not subject to an electoral threshold.&#x20;

On the right side of the Turkish [political spectrum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_spectrum), parties like the [Democrat Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democrat_Party_\(Turkey,_1946%E2%80%931961\)), [Justice Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justice_Party_\(Turkey\)), [Motherland Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motherland_Party_\(Turkey\)), and [Justice and Development Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Justice_and_Development_Party_\(Turkey\)) became the most popular political parties in Turkey, winning numerous elections. Turkish [right-wing](56522.md) parties are more likely to embrace the principles of political ideologies such as [conservatism](6675.md), [nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalism) or [Islamism](15012.md).[^cite_note-241] On the left side of the spectrum, parties like the [Republican People's Party](404748.md), [Social Democratic Populist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_Democratic_Populist_Party) and [Democratic Left Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Left_Party_\(Turkey\)) once enjoyed the largest electoral success. [Left-wing](18499.md) parties are more likely to embrace the principles of [socialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialism), [Kemalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kemalism) or [secularism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secularism).[^cite_note-FleetFaroqhi2008p357-242]&#x20;

The Constitutional Court can strip the public financing of [political parties](396397.md) that it deems [anti-secular](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secularism_in_Turkey) or having ties to [terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrorism_in_Turkey), or ban their existence altogether.[^cite_note-243][^cite_note-244] On 20 May 2016, the Turkish parliament stripped almost a quarter of its members of immunity from prosecution, including 101 deputies from the pro-Kurdish [HDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peoples%27_Democratic_Party_\(Turkey\)) and the main opposition [CHP](404748.md) party.[^cite_note-245][^cite_note-european_court-246] In its 2023 report, the [European Commission](9974.md) criticized how democratic institutions in Turkey operate.[^cite_note-247] The criticism was rejected by Turkey.[^cite_note-248]&#x20;

### Law

Main articles: [Judicial system of Turkey](2963429.md), [Law enforcement in Turkey](6410080.md), and [Hate speech laws in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hate_speech_laws_in_Turkey)

With the founding of the Republic, Turkey adopted a [civil law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_law_\(legal_system\)) legal system, replacing [Sharia](28840.md)-derived [Ottoman law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_law). The [Civil Code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_civil_code_\(1926\)), adopted in 1926, was based on the [Swiss Civil Code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swiss_Civil_Code) of 1907 and the [Swiss Code of Obligations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swiss_Code_of_Obligations) of 1911. Although it underwent a number of changes in 2002, it retains much of the basis of the original Code. The [Criminal Code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criminal_Code), originally based on the [Italian Criminal Code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_law_codes#Penal_Code), was replaced in 2005 by a Code with principles similar to the [German Penal Code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Penal_Code) and German law generally. [Administrative law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_law) is based on the French equivalent and [procedural law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Procedural_law) generally shows the influence of the Swiss, German and French legal systems.[^cite_note-249] Islamic principles do not play a part in the legal system.[^cite_note-250]&#x20;

[Law enforcement in Turkey](6410080.md) is carried out by several agencies under the jurisdiction of the [Ministry of Internal Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_the_Interior_\(Turkey\)). These agencies are the [General Directorate of Security](20141676.md), the [Gendarmerie General Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gendarmerie_General_Command) and the [Coast Guard Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coast_Guard_Command).[^cite_note-251] In the years of government by the Justice and Development Party and Erdoğan, particularly since 2013, the independence and integrity of the Turkish judiciary has increasingly been said to be in doubt by institutions, parliamentarians and journalists both within and outside of Turkey, because of political interference in the promotion of judges and prosecutors and in their pursuit of public duty.[^cite_note-EU2015-252][^cite_note-EP2016-253][^cite_note-TI2016-254]&#x20;

### Foreign relations

Main article: [Foreign relations of Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Turkey)



Turkey has been a member of [NATO](21133.md) since 1952, has its second largest army and is the host of the [Allied Land Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allied_Land_Command) headquarters.

Turkey has been characterized as an [emerging](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emerging_power),[^cite_note-Acharya_2014-255] rising,[^cite_note-256] [middle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_power),[^cite_note-257] quasi-regional,[^cite_note-258] and a [regional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_power) power.[^cite_note-259][^cite_note-260] Turkey's constant foreign policy goal is to pursue its national interests. These interests are mainly growing the economy, and maintaining security from [internal terrorist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrorism_in_Turkey) and external threats.[^cite_note-261] After the establishment of the Republic, [Atatürk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atat%C3%BCrk) and [İnönü](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%B0smet_%C4%B0n%C3%B6n%C3%BC) followed the "[peace at home, peace in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_at_home,_peace_in_the_world)" principle until the [Cold War](325329.md)'s start.[^cite_note-Martin_2012-262] Following [threats from the Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_territorial_claims_against_Turkey), Turkey sought to [ally with the United States](5380399.md) and joined [NATO](21133.md) in 1952.[^cite_note-263][^cite_note-Martin_2012-262]&#x20;

Overall, Turkey aims for good relations with [Central Asia](6742.md), the [Caucasus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caucasus), Russia, the [Middle East](19323.md), and Iran. With the West, Turkey also aims to keep its arrangements.[^cite_note-Martin_2012_234–235-264] By trading with the east and joining the EU, Turkey pursues economic growth.[^cite_note-Martin_2012_234–235-264] Turkey joined the [European Union Customs Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Union_Customs_Union) in 1995,[^cite_note-265] but [its EU accession talks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accession_of_Turkey_to_the_European_Union) are frozen.[^cite_note-266]&#x20;

Turkey has sought closer relations with the Central Asian Turkic states after the breakup of the Soviet Union.[^cite_note-Martin_2012_230-267] Closer [relations with Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azerbaijan%E2%80%93Turkey_relations), a culturally close country, was achieved.[^cite_note-Martin_2012_230-267] Turkey is a founding member of the [International Organization of Turkic Culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Organization_of_Turkic_Culture) and [Organization of Turkic States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organization_of_Turkic_States).[^cite_note-268][^cite_note-269] It is also a member of [Organization for Security and Cooperation in Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organization_for_Security_and_Cooperation_in_Europe), [Council of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Europe), and [Organisation of Islamic Cooperation](47725.md).[^cite_note-270]&#x20;

Following the [Arab Spring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Spring), Turkey had problems with countries such as United Arab Emirates, Saudi Arabia, and Egypt.[^cite_note-Reuters_19_May_2023-271] Relations with these countries have improved since then.[^cite_note-Reuters_19_May_2023-271][^cite_note-272][^cite_note-273] There are disputes [with Greece over maritime boundaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aegean_dispute) and [with Cyprus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyprus%E2%80%93Turkey_maritime_zones_dispute).[^cite_note-274] In 2018, the Turkish military and the Turkish-backed forces began an [operation in Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Olive_Branch) aimed at ousting US-backed [YPG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YPG) (which Turkey considers to be an offshoot of the outlawed [PKK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PKK))[^cite_note-Atlantic-Council-275][^cite_note-Ministry_Foreign_Affairs_statement-171] from the enclave of [Afrin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afrin,_Syria).[^cite_note-276][^cite_note-TRT-World-25-05-2022-277] Turkey has also conducted airstrikes in [Iraqi Kurdistan](679693.md), which was criticized by Iraq for violating its sovereignty and killing civilians.[^cite_note-278] [Diplomatic relations with Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Turkey_relations) were damaged after the [2010 Gaza flotilla raid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Gaza_flotilla_raid),[^cite_note-279] normalized in 2016,[^cite_note-280] and cut again following the [Israeli invasion of the Gaza Strip](74998519.md).[^cite_note-Reuters_28_May_2024-281] In 2024, Turkey stopped trading with Israel.[^cite_note-Reuters_28_May_2024-281]&#x20;

### Military

Main article: [Turkish Armed Forces](30205.md)

See also: [Military history of Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_history_of_Turkey), [Turkish Land Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Land_Forces), [Turkish Naval Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Naval_Forces), and [Turkish Air Force](1799517.md)



The [TAI TF Kaan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TAI_TF_Kaan) is currently being developed by [Turkish Aerospace Industries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Aerospace_Industries) for the [Turkish Air Force](1799517.md).

[^cite_note-282]

[^cite_note-283]

[^cite_note-284]

The [Turkish Armed Forces](30205.md) are responsible for defense against foreign threats. Although the commander-in-chief is the president, the [General Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Staff_of_the_Turkish_Armed_Forces), [Air Force](1799517.md), [Naval Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Naval_Forces), and [Land Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Land_Forces) usually report to the Minister of National Defence.[^cite_note-285] The [Gendarmerie General Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gendarmerie_General_Command) and the [Coast Guard Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coast_Guard_Command) are under the jurisdiction of the [Ministry of the Interior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_the_Interior_\(Turkey\)).[^cite_note-286] [Military service is required](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conscription_in_Turkey) for 6–12 months for men,[^cite_note-287] which is reduced to one month after paying a fee.[^cite_note-288] Turkey does not recognize [conscientious objection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conscientious_objection) and does not offer a [civilian alternative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alternative_civilian_service) to military service.[^cite_note-289]&#x20;

Turkey has the [second-largest military force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Member_states_of_NATO#Military_personnel) in NATO, after the [United States](32212.md), with an estimated strength of 890,700 military personnel as of February 2022.[^cite_note-iiss-mb-2022-290] Turkey is considered a significant power in [unmanned aerial vehicles](58900.md).[^cite_note-291] As part of the [nuclear sharing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_sharing) policy of NATO, Turkey hosts approximately 20 United States [B61 nuclear bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B61_nuclear_bomb) at the [Incirlik Air Base](1597543.md).[^cite_note-292][^cite_note-293] In recent years, [Turkey's defense industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_industry_of_Turkey) has developed rapidly.[^cite_note-294] [Aselsan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aselsan), [Turkish Aerospace Industries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Aerospace_Industries), [Roketsan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roketsan), and [ASFAT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ASFAT) are among the top 100 defense companies in the world.[^cite_note-295]&#x20;

Turkey has participated in international missions under the United Nations and NATO [since the Korean War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Brigade), including [peacekeeping](84617.md) missions in [Somalia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Operation_in_Somalia_I), [Yugoslavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Protection_Force) and the [Horn of Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Ocean_Shield). It supported [coalition forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coalition_of_the_Gulf_War) in the [First Gulf War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_War), contributed military personnel to the [International Security Assistance Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Security_Assistance_Force) in Afghanistan, and remains active in [Kosovo Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kosovo_Force), [Eurocorps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurocorps) and [EU Battlegroups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EU_Battlegroup).[^cite_note-Enter_the_EU_Battle_Groups-296][^cite_note-tskpeace-297] As of 2016, Turkey has assisted [Peshmerga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peshmerga) forces in northern [Iraq](7515928.md) and the [Somali Armed Forces](27366.md) with security and training.[^cite_note-hurriyetdailynews.comq-298][^cite_note-reuters.comq-299] The Turkish Armed Forces have a relatively substantial military presence abroad,[^cite_note-300] with [military bases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_with_overseas_military_bases) in [Albania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pasha_Liman_Base),[^cite_note-LarrabeeLesser94-301] [Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraq%E2%80%93Turkey_relations#Turkish_military_presence_in_Iraq),[^cite_note-302] [Qatar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qatar%E2%80%93Turkey_relations#Turkish_military_presence_in_Qatar),[^cite_note-303] and [Somalia](55425081.md).[^cite_note-304] The country has had [36,000 troops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_military_forces_in_Northern_Cyprus) in [Northern Cyprus](271409.md) since 1974.[^cite_note-Richmond1998-305]&#x20;

### Human rights

Main article: [Human rights in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_Turkey)



Women demonstrating and asking for non-interference with their clothing in [Kadıköy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kad%C4%B1k%C3%B6y), [Istanbul](3391396.md)

[^cite_note-306]

The Constitution includes references to upholding the rule of law and human rights,[^cite_note-307] and says that international human rights conventions supersede national law.[^cite_note-308] But Turkey has far more cases pending at the [European Court of Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Court_of_Human_Rights) (ECHR) than any other country,[^cite_note-309]<sup>: 7 </sup> and in high profile cases sometimes ignores ECHR judgements.[^cite_note-310][^cite_note-311] The [Human Rights and Equality Institution of Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Rights_and_Equality_Institution_of_Turkey) is the government body responsible for protecting human rights, but the EU says that its effectiveness and that of [the ombudsman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ombudsman_Institution) is limited.[^cite_note-312]<sup>: 32 </sup>&#x20;

The UN has criticised the government for keeping some pollution information from the public.[^cite_note-313] Civil society organisation [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) and others say that the government uses the justice system against opposition politicians.[^cite_note-314][^cite_note-315]&#x20;

In the 2000s, legal changes were made for public use of and teaching in the Kurdish language. This included opening a [Kurdish-language national TV channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TRT_Kurd%C3%AE). Various "openings" were made to address concerns of minorities such as [Alevi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alevi), [ethnic Kurds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kurds_in_Turkey), and [ethnic Romani people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romani_people_in_Turkey).[^cite_note-Toprak_2012_p222-316] Sentences for violence against women were strengthened.[^cite_note-Toprak_2012_p222-316]&#x20;

In 2013, [widespread protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gezi_Park_protests) erupted, sparked by a plan to demolish [Gezi Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gezi_Park) but soon growing into general anti-government dissent.[^cite_note-317]&#x20;

Armenians, Greeks and Jews have at times been portrayed as undesirable or unwelcome groups (including by senior government officials) and subjected to [racial slurs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racial_slurs).[^cite_note-\:1-181]&#x20;

### LGBTQ+ issues

Main articles: [LGBTQ rights in Turkey](4750738.md) and [2026 Turkish LGBTQ raids](84248927.md)

Under the Republic, gay and lesbian sex has never been criminalized.[^cite_note-318] However, LGBT people in Turkey face discrimination, harassment and even violence.[^cite_note-319] With the first annual [Istanbul Pride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Istanbul_Pride) in 2003, Turkey became the first Muslim-majority country to hold a gay pride march.[^cite_note-320] Since 2015, parades at [Taksim Square](631961.md) and [İstiklal Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%B0stiklal_Avenue) have been denied government permission, citing security concerns, but hundreds of people have defied the ban each year.[^cite_note-Bianet_–_Bagimsiz_Iletisim_Agi-321] The bans were criticized,[^cite_note-Bianet_–_Bagimsiz_Iletisim_Agi-321] and the [Turkish Human Rights Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Human_Rights_Association) says the government is targeting the LGBTQ+ community.[^cite_note-322]&#x20;

On the morning of 12 September 2026, police started raiding LGBTQ+ clubs and unions. Turkish police detained many people, and over 100 of them were held by regional courts. The police also raided Turkey's longest-running LGBTQ+ advocacy group, [KAOS GL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KAOS_GL), and detained its board of directors. [Justice Minister](10283737.md) [Akın Gürlek](82385461.md) announced on social media that 13 businesses and 9 associations had been raided.[^cite_note-323][^cite_note-324][^cite_note-325] Along with the raids, Turkish courts issued rulings to block the social media accounts of LGBTQ+ advocacy groups, human rights organizations, newspapers, and journalists,[^cite_note-326][^cite_note-327][^cite_note-328] including groups such as [Amnesty International](18947898.md), along with women's advocacy groups.[^cite_note-329]&#x20;

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Turkey)

See also: [Economic history of Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_history_of_Turkey) and [Tourism in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Turkey)



Turkey's nominal [gross domestic product](12594.md) between 1960 and 2023 in 2015 [constant US dollars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constant_dollars)

[^cite_note-330]

[^cite_note-331]

Turkey is an [upper-middle](1242956.md)-income country and an [emerging market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emerging_market).[^cite_note-WorldBank_Overview_April_2024-204][^cite_note-332] A founding member of the [OECD](33853117.md) and [G20](2206608.md), it has the [16th-largest economy by nominal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(nominal\)) and [11th-largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(PPP\)) by [PPP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity)-adjusted GDP in the world.[^cite_note-IMFWEO.TR-8] It is classified among [newly industrialized countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newly_industrialized_countries). The [service sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Service_sector) accounts for 61% of the economy, whereas industry accounts for 32% and agriculture contributes about 7%.[^cite_note-333] According to [IMF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IMF) estimates, Turkey's GDP per capita by PPP is \$43,790 in 2025, while its nominal GDP per capita is \$18,200.[^cite_note-IMFWEO.TR-8] Potential growth is weakened by long-lasting structural and macro obstacles, such as slow rates of productivity growth and high inflation.[^cite_note-WorldBank_Overview_April_2024-204]&#x20;

Turkey has a diversified economy; main industries include automobiles, electronics, textiles, construction, steel, mining, and food processing.[^cite_note-World_Factbook_Economy-334] Machinery and manufacturing lead among products in Turkey's merchandise exports.[^cite_note-335] Turkey is a [major agricultural producer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_producing_countries_of_agricultural_commodities).[^cite_note-336] It ranks [8th in crude steel production](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_steel_production), and [13th in motor vehicle production](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_motor_vehicle_production), ship building (by [tonnage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tonnage)), and annual industrial robot installation in the world.[^cite_note-337] [Turkish automative companies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automotive_industry_in_Turkey) include [TEMSA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TEMSA), [Otokar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Otokar), [BMC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BMC_\(Turkey\)) and [Togg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Togg). Togg is the first all-electric vehicle company of Turkey. [Arçelik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ar%C3%A7elik), [Vestel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vestel), and [Beko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beko) are major manufacturers of consumer electronics.[^cite_note-338] Arçelik is one of the largest producers of household goods in the world.[^cite_note-339] In 2022, Turkey ranked second in the world in terms of the number of international contractors in the top 250 list.[^cite_note-340]&#x20;



[Bodrum](306419.md) in [Turkish Riviera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_Riviera). Bodrum peninsula is a high-end spot for vacationers.

[^cite_note-341]

Turkey's services exports are mostly transport and tourism.[^cite_note-342] Tourism accounts for about 8% of Turkey's GDP.[^cite_note-343] In 2024, Turkey [ranked fourth in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Tourism_rankings#Most_visited_destinations_by_international_tourist_arrivals) in the number of international tourist arrivals with 60.6 million foreign tourists.[^cite_note-344] Turkey has [22 UNESCO World Heritage Sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_World_Heritage_Sites_in_Turkey) and [84 World Heritage Sites in tentative list](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_World_Heritage_Sites_in_Turkey_\(Tentative_list\)). Turkey is home to 625 [Blue Flag beaches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Flag_beaches), third most in the world.[^cite_note-345] In 2024, [Euromonitor International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euromonitor_International) ranked [Istanbul](3391396.md) and [Antalya](361527.md) among [the top ten most visited cities in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_by_international_visitors).[^cite_note-346] [Turkish Airlines](167925.md) is one of the [largest airlines in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Largest_airlines_in_the_world).&#x20;

Between 2007 and 2021, the share of population below the PPP-\$6.85 per day [international poverty threshold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty_threshold) declined from 20% to 7.6%.[^cite_note-WorldBank_Overview_April_2024-204] In 2023, 13.9% of the population was below the national at-risk-of-poverty rate.[^cite_note-347] In 2021, 34% of the population were at risk of poverty or social exclusion, using [Eurostat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurostat) definition.[^cite_note-348] Unemployment in Turkey was 10.4% in 2022.[^cite_note-349] In 2021, it was estimated that 47% of total disposable income was received by the top 20% of income earners, while the lowest 20% received only 6%.[^cite_note-350] Compared to the OECD average, [labor force participation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labor_force_participation) of women is lower. Affordable [childcare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Childcare) and better [parental leave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parental_leave) policies are needed to lift women's employment.[^cite_note-OECD_2025-2-227]&#x20;

### Infrastructure

Main articles: [Energy in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_Turkey), [Renewable energy in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renewable_energy_in_Turkey), and [Transport in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Turkey)

See also: [Communications in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communications_in_Turkey) and [Water supply and sanitation in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_supply_and_sanitation_in_Turkey)



Installed electricity capacity in Turkey between 2000 and 2024 in terms of gigawatts

[^cite_note-351]

Turkey is the [15th-largest electricity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_electricity_production) and [13th-largest renewable electricity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_renewable_electricity_production) producer in the world. [Turkey's energy generation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_Turkey) capacity increased significantly, [its electricity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electricity_sector_in_Turkey) is about half [from renewable sources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renewable_energy_in_Turkey).[^cite_note-352][^cite_note-353] Turkey is also the [fourth-largest producer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geothermal_power#Utility-grade_stations) of [geothermal power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geothermal_energy_in_Turkey) in the world.[^cite_note-2019_Capacity-354] Turkey's first nuclear power station, [Akkuyu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akkuyu_Nuclear_Power_Plant), will increase diversification of its energy mix.[^cite_note-IEA_2021-355] When it comes to [total final consumption](67895571.md#Final_consumption), fossil fuels still play a large role, accounting for 73%.[^cite_note-356] A major reason of [Turkey's greenhouse gas emissions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkey%27s_greenhouse_gas_emissions) is the [large proportion of coal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coal_in_Turkey) in the energy system.[^cite_note-357] While the government has invested in low carbon energy transition, there are some [fossil fuel subsidies](44334234.md#Turkey) until at least 2030.[^cite_note-358][^cite_note-359] By 2053, Turkey aims to have [net zero emissions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Net_zero_emissions).[^cite_note-World_Bank_Türkiye-226][^cite_note-OECD_2025-2-227]&#x20;



[Istanbul Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Istanbul_Airport) is one of the [top 10 busiest airports in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_busiest_airports_by_passenger_traffic).

Turkey has made [energy security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_security) a top priority, given its heavy reliance on gas and oil imports.[^cite_note-IEA_2021-355] Turkey's main energy supply sources are [Russia](14049009.md), West Asia, and Central Asia.[^cite_note-Martin_2012_234–235-264] [Gas production](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gas_in_Turkey) began in 2023 in the recently discovered [Sakarya gas field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sakarya_gas_field). When fully operational, it will supply about 30% of the natural gas needed domestically.[^cite_note-360][^cite_note-361] Turkey aims to become a hub for regional energy transportation.[^cite_note-Novikau_Muhasilović_2023-362] Several oil and gas pipelines span the country, including the [Blue Stream](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Stream), [TurkStream](45206475.md), and [Baku-Tbilisi-Ceyhan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baku-Tbilisi-Ceyhan) pipelines.[^cite_note-Novikau_Muhasilović_2023-362]&#x20;

As of 2023, Turkey has 3,726 kilometers of [controlled-access highways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Otoyol) and 29,373 kilometers of [divided highways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Divided_highways).[^cite_note-363] Multiple bridges and tunnels connect Asian and European sides of Turkey; the [Çanakkale 1915 Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%87anakkale_1915_Bridge) on the [Dardanelles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dardanelles) strait is the [longest suspension bridge in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_longest_suspension_bridge_spans#Completed_suspension_bridges).[^cite_note-364] [Marmaray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marmaray) and [Eurasia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurasia_Tunnel) tunnels under the [Bosporus](3705.md) connect both sides of Istanbul.[^cite_note-365] The [Osman Gazi Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osman_Gazi_Bridge) connects the northern and southern shores of the [Gulf of İzmit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_of_%C4%B0zmit).&#x20;

[Turkish State Railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_State_Railways) operates both conventional and high speed trains, with the government expanding both.[^cite_note-366] [High-speed rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-speed_rail_in_Turkey) lines include the [Ankara-Istanbul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ankara-Istanbul_high-speed_railway), [Ankara-Konya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ankara-Konya_high-speed_railway), and [Ankara-Sivas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ankara%E2%80%93Sivas_high-speed_railway) routes.[^cite_note-367] [Istanbul Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Istanbul_Metro) is the largest subway network in the country with around 704 million annual ridership in 2019.[^cite_note-368] There are [115 airports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_Turkey) as of 2024.[^cite_note-CIA_transport-369] [Istanbul Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Istanbul_Airport) is one of the [top 10 busiest airports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_busiest_airports_by_passenger_traffic) in the world. Turkey aims to become a transportation hub.[^cite_note-370][^cite_note-Atlı_2018-371] It is part of various routes that connect Asia and Europe, including [the Middle Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trans-Caspian_International_Transport_Route).[^cite_note-Atlı_2018-371] In 2024, Turkey, Iraq, UAE, and Qatar signed an agreement to link Iraqi port facilities to Turkey via road and rail connections.[^cite_note-372]&#x20;

There is a [lack of open data](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Access_to_public_information_in_Turkey).[^cite_note-373]&#x20;

### Science and technology

Main article: [Science and technology in Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_Turkey)

See also: [Scientific and Technological Research Council of Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scientific_and_Technological_Research_Council_of_Turkey)



[Göktürk-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G%C3%B6kt%C3%BCrk-1), [Göktürk-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G%C3%B6kt%C3%BCrk-2) and [Göktürk-3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G%C3%B6kt%C3%BCrk-3) are the [Earth observation satellites](46597.md) of the [Turkish Ministry of National Defense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_National_Defense_\(Turkey\)), while state-owned [Türksat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%C3%BCrksat_\(company\)) operates the [Türksat series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%C3%BCrksat_\(satellite\)) of [communications satellites](45207.md).

Turkey's spending on [research and development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Research_and_development) as a share of GDP has risen from 0.47% in 2000 to 1.40% in 2023,[^cite_note-374] although it is still around half of the average for the OECD.[^cite_note-OECD_2025-375] [Turkey ranks 16th in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_number_of_scientific_and_technical_journal_articles) in terms of article output in scientific and technical journals, and 35th in [Nature Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nature_Index).[^cite_note-376][^cite_note-377] Turkish patent office ranks 21st worldwide in overall patent applications, and 3rd in industrial design applications. Vast majority of applicants to the Turkish patent office are Turkish residents. In all patent offices globally, [Turkish residents rank 21st for overall patent applications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Intellectual_Property_Indicators#Latest_filing_trends_by_country_of_origin).[^cite_note-WIPO_Türkiye-378] In 2025, Turkey ranked 43rd in the world in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index).[^cite_note-379][^cite_note-380] [TÜBİTAK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%C3%9CB%C4%B0TAK) is one of the main agencies for funding and carrying out research.[^cite_note-381][^cite_note-euraxess_Türkiye-382][^cite_note-383]&#x20;

Although Turkey needs more national innovation,[^cite_note-384] it is improving in areas such as high technology and defense.[^cite_note-OECD_2025-375] To boost the output of high-[value-added](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Value-added) products, Turkey launched its National Technology Initiative in 2019.[^cite_note-385] In line with its research priorities, Turkey developed roadmaps in various areas such as [advanced materials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Materials_science), [artificial intelligence](1164.md), [biotechnology](4502.md), [internet of things](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_of_things), [nanoelectronics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanoelectronics), [robotics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robotics), and [quantum technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quantum_technology).[^cite_note-euraxess_Türkiye-382] Some of the main science and technology programs include the National Research Program, Industry and Technology Strategy, Climate Change Strategy, National Artificial Intelligence Strategy, and [National Space Program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Space_program_of_Turkey).[^cite_note-euraxess_Türkiye-382] In 2024 and 2025, Turkey's first [electron accelerator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electron_accelerator),[^cite_note-386] [quantum computer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quantum_computer),[^cite_note-387] and [communication satellite manufactured domestically](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%C3%BCrksat_6A) became operational.[^cite_note-388]&#x20;

## Demographics

Main article: [Demographics of Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Turkey)

See also: [Turkish people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkish_people)

According to the *Address-Based Population Recording System*, the country's population was 86 million in 2025, excluding Syrians under temporary protection.[^cite_note-Population_of_Turkey-7] 94% lived in [province and district centers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_area).[^cite_note-Population_of_Turkey-7] People within the 15–64 and 0–14 age groups corresponded to 69% and 20% of the total population, respectively. Those aged 65 years or older made up 11%.[^cite_note-Population_of_Turkey-7] Between 1950 and 2020, Turkey's population more than quadrupled from 20.9 million to 83.6 million;[^cite_note-389] however, the population growth rate was 0.1% in 2023.[^cite_note-Population_of_Turkey-7] In 2023, the [total fertility rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Total_fertility_rate) was 1.51 children per woman, [below the replacement rate of 2.10 per woman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sub-replacement_fertility).[^cite_note-390]&#x20;
